DEVOS-134 (Execution-path preview) is also COMPLETE, per explicit user approval ("proceed") — a new, pure `computeExecutionPaths` function (`packages/domain/src/workflows/compute-execution-paths.ts`) traverses a graph's real declared edges (the same edges `run-creation.ts` already treats as real ordering) to enumerate distinct paths, correctly branching at `CONDITION`/`PARALLEL` nodes and treating `JOIN` as a pass-through; a real, disclosed narrowing of the Designer spec's own "happy/failure" taxonomy (this engine has no "failure path" concept — paths are labeled only from real graph facts: branch keys taken, and whether they pass through `APPROVAL`/`PARALLEL`/`JOIN`). Proven with a real unit test mirroring Sprint 12's own Incident Response graph shape exactly (a `CONDITION` with two branches, a `PARALLEL`/`JOIN` fan-out, an `APPROVAL` node) — correctly produces 3 distinct paths with correct labels; a cyclic-graph test confirms no infinite loop. A new `WorkflowPathPreview.tsx` renders this in `WorkflowsPage.tsx` before save/publish, importing `computeExecutionPaths` directly from `@devos/domain` (the same real, already-proven-safe direct-import pattern DEVOS-131 established). DEVOS-133 (Version diff) is also COMPLETE, per explicit user approval ("proceed") — a new, pure `diffWorkflowVersions` function (`packages/domain/src/workflows/diff-workflow-versions.ts`) computes a real structural diff between two `WorkflowVersion` graphs (nodes added/removed/changed by id, edges added/removed by `(from,to,branch)`, policies/outputs added/removed), proven with 3 real unit tests including the exact acceptance scenario (an added node, a removed edge, and a changed `CONDITION` node's `config.rule` — reported exactly and nothing else). A new `WorkflowVersionDiffView.tsx` renders it in `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s new "Compare versions" section (two version selectors, populated from the workflow's own real `listWorkflowVersions`), importing `diffWorkflowVersions` directly from `@devos/domain` (the same real, already-proven-safe direct-import pattern DEVOS-131/134 established). DEVOS-135 (Workflow library page) is also COMPLETE, per explicit user approval ("proceed") — a real, modest extension to `listWorkflowDefinitionsForProject` (`packages/application/src/workflows/list-workflow-definitions.ts`) composes each definition's `latestVersionStatus`/`versionCount` from the already-existing `workflowVersions.listForDefinition` (no new table/migration); a new `ListWorkflowRunsForDefinition` domain type (`packages/domain/src/workflows/workflow-run.ts`) plus `createWorkflowRunsForDefinitionLister` (`packages/database/src/repositories/workflow-runs.ts`, a real join against `workflow_versions`) power a new `GET /workflows/:workflowId/runs` route — a separate flat type/deps interface (`ListWorkflowRunsForDefinitionDeps`), the same precedent as `AgentExecutionSummaryUseCaseDeps`/`ToolInvocationSummaryUseCaseDeps`, deliberately not added to `WorkflowRunRepository` itself after grepping and finding 21 existing fakes of that interface that a widening would have broken; a new `WorkflowLibraryPage.tsx` lists every real workflow across the user's projects, searchable/filterable by project/project-type/status, with a real run-health summary (`RUN_TERMINAL_STATUSES`, the same terminal-status set `RunsPage.tsx` already established) and expandable version history; a new "Clone into new draft" action (new `createWorkflow` client wrapper — the first UI caller of the existing, unmodified `createWorkflowDefinition` use case outside the project-type clone pipeline) copies a source definition's latest version graph verbatim into a new `WorkflowDefinition`, in the same or a different project. **One real bug found and fixed during implementation:** the new `listRunsForDefinition` wiring was originally unconditional in `apps/api/src/app.ts`'s route composition, which crashed (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'selectFrom')`) against `apps/api/tests/app.test.ts`'s fake/null database client — fixed by making it injectable via `CreateAppOptions.listRunsForDefinition`, mirroring how `database` itself is already overridable, with a real in-memory implementation added to the test file's own `createInMemoryWorkflowDeps`. Live-verified against a real running `apps/api` and real Postgres: a real project's cloned workflows show correct real `latestVersionStatus`/`versionCount`; a real started run appears via the new runs endpoint; a real clone-into-new-draft produced a real new `WorkflowDefinition` with the source graph copied verbatim, confirmed via a direct Postgres query; all test data (project, workflows, versions, runs, agents, work items, audit records, outbox events) fully cleaned up afterward (confirmed 0 remaining rows). Full validation: `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint build test --filter='!@devos/e2e-tests'` 76/76 green; the full real `tests/e2e` suite re-confirmed unaffected (16/16 files, 37/37 tests green, after one transient worker-startup timeout flake on the first run was independently re-run and confirmed clean). DEVOS-137 (Real end-to-end pilot: author, publish, run) is also COMPLETE, per explicit user approval ("proceed") — a brand-new, never-seeded `WorkflowDefinition` ("DEVOS-137 Pilot Workflow") was created against a real running `apps/api` and real Postgres, driving the exact API contract the visual designer's own actions use: `createWorkflowDefinition` (minimal starting graph, one `TASK` node) → `updateDraftWorkflow` (the real authored graph — `route` `CONDITION` → `fanout` `PARALLEL` → `branch-a`/`branch-b` `TASK`s → `join` `JOIN` (tolerant) → `wait` `WAIT` (duration) → `gate` `APPROVAL` → `final` `TASK`, plus an untaken `not-taken` `TASK` on the `CONDITION`'s false branch) → `validateDraftWorkflow` (zero issues) → `publishWorkflowVersion` (a real immutable `PUBLISHED` v1, its `definition` confirmed to match exactly what was authored). A real run was started with `{proceed: true}` against a real running `apps/worker` process (no `AGENT_TASK`/invented `TOOL_TASK` — only the six node types a real worker can already dispatch with zero new registration, per this task's own real scoping decision) and reached `COMPLETED` for real: every task `SUCCEEDED` (`route`, `fanout`, `branch-a`, `branch-b`, `join`, `wait`, the real mid-graph `APPROVAL` request at `gate` — decided through the existing, unchanged DEVOS-110/111 approve path — and `final`) except the untaken `CONDITION` branch (`not-taken`), which correctly reached `SKIPPED`. Confirmed independently via a direct Postgres query (not just the API), then all test data (project, workflow definition/version, run, tasks, approval, work item) fully cleaned up (confirmed 0 remaining rows). **Sprint 14 (DEVOS-133–137, Lifecycle, Preview & Library) is COMPLETE — the whole E21 Workflow Designer epic's own exit criterion is now closed with real evidence: a user can go from a blank canvas to a real, running workflow without ever touching a database seed script, a raw JSON body, or a developer.** The E19 gate pilot (`specs/DEVOS-POST-POC-BACKLOG-AND-SPRINT-PLAN.md` §9) has since been run and completed for real — see the 2026-08-31 state-change-log entry below. |

Also found and fixed genuine Prettier formatting drift across 61 files (not a CRLF artifact — confirmed by grepping for literal `\r`) via `pnpm format`, re-verified with a full 79/79 `typecheck`/`lint`/`test`/`build` pass afterward. **Branch-protection required-status-checks enforcement (the task's other acceptance half) could not be configured** — this repository has no `git remote`, so there is no GitHub repository to apply branch protection to; the exact `gh api` command is documented in `DEVOS-SPRINT1-DECISIONS.md` for the user to run once a remote exists. | DEVOS-024 — Sprint Hardening & Demo                                    | COMPLETE                   | New `TaskQueue.reclaimStale()` port + Postgres implementation (`packages/domain`, `packages/database`), wired into `apps/worker`'s dispatcher (reclaim on start + every 60s, 5-minute stale threshold) — closes the "worker crashes mid-task, stuck forever" gap. New `tests/e2e/hardening.test.ts`: proves no duplicate task delivery under concurrent `claimNext()`, and that a stale `RUNNING` task is reclaimed and correctly re-attempted or exhausted to `FAILED` per `MAX_TASK_ATTEMPTS`. Found and fixed two real bugs during this task: a Turborepo 2.x strict-env-filtering bug that silently broke `pnpm dev` (the actual documented top-level command, never exercised end-to-end before this task's clean-environment demo pass) via a new `globalEnv` in `turbo.json`; and cross-test-file/cross-test interference against the shared live Postgres queue, fixed with a package-local `tests/e2e/vitest.config.ts` (`fileParallelism: false`) and proper terminal-state cleanup in every hardening test. New `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` documents Sprint 1's known scope boundaries, including the reclaim mechanism's own heartbeat-vs-polling limitation. Full forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; clean-environment demo (`pnpm bootstrap` → `pnpm dev` → `pnpm smoke` → full project→run→artifact→audit cycle via curl) verified working end-to-end. **Sprint 1 is COMPLETE.** | DEVOS-027 — Implement LLM Provider Adapter                             | COMPLETE                   | New `createGeminiModelAdapter()` in `packages/agents` — plain REST calls via `fetch` (no SDK dependency added; keeps the adapter boundary free of provider-specific types leaking further into the codebase), requesting JSON-mode output, parsing candidate text into a structured result, separating a top-level `uncertainty` array, and mapping HTTP failures/blocked prompts/non-STOP finish reasons to `FAILED` results with clear messages. Provider = Google Gemini (free tier), a project decision (not from the historical spec) made when Sprint 2 was scoped. **Live-verified against the real Gemini API** using the user's key: first attempt correctly surfaced a real 404 (`gemini-2.0-flash` deprecated), second attempt against `gemini-3.6-flash` (the model Google's own error suggested) succeeded with a correctly parsed JSON response. Found and fixed a real, previously-latent CI-reliability bug while validating this task: `turbo.json`'s `typecheck` task depended on `^typecheck` instead of `^build`, so a downstream package's `tsc --noEmit` could race against an upstream package's `dist/` not existing yet (upstream `package.json` `main`/`types` point at `dist/`, which only `build` produces) — this had silently worked all session because `dist/` was usually already warm from earlier manual builds; a genuinely clean run exposed it. Fixed by changing `typecheck`'s `dependsOn` to `["^build"]`, matching how `test` already depends on `^build`; re-verified reliable across two consecutive clean, forced, no-cache full validation runs (79/79 both times). | DEVOS-035 — Connect Agents to Workflow                                 | COMPLETE                   | Wires the four planning-path agents into `apps/worker`'s real dispatch table for the `AGENT_TASK` node type, alongside (not replacing — Sprint 1's own e2e/hardening suites still exercise it) the untouched `TASK` → `runDiscoveryTask` registration. New `apps/worker/src/agent-task-router.ts` (`routeAgentTask`) resolves which of DEVOS-031–034's handlers actually runs, keyed by the task's `agentRef` — the only mechanism available, since there is one `AGENT_TASK` `WorkflowNodeType`, not four. Found and fixed a real, previously-latent correctness gap while building this: sibling tasks in one run all shared the exact same `createdAt` (already flagged, unaddressed, in `hardening.test.ts`'s own code comment), so `claimNext()`'s `ORDER BY created_at ASC` gave no guarantee which claimed first — harmless while every Sprint 1 workflow had at most one task per run, but fatal for a 4-stage chain where each stage needs the previous one's artifact to exist first. Fixed in `run-creation.ts` with a one-millisecond-per-node offset (node declaration order becomes deterministic claim order) without touching the queue/claim logic itself. New seeded 4-node `planning-path` workflow definition/version (`SEED_PLANNING_PATH_WORKFLOW_*` in `seed-constants.ts`/`seed.ts`) chaining discovery→requirements→technical-design→planning via `agentRef`. New `GEMINI_API_KEY` support in `@devos/config` (optional in the shared schema — apps/api has no LLM dependency; only apps/worker enforces it, and only for the `AGENT_TASK` handler specifically, not the whole process). New `apps/worker/tests/agent-task-router.test.ts` (5 cases: one per agent key routing correctly, plus an unrecognized-agentRef failure). **Found and fixed a real regression during this task's own validation**: an initial fail-fast-the-whole-process-on-missing-`GEMINI_API_KEY` design broke DEVOS-021's e2e vertical-slice test, which spawns a real worker process with no LLM credential configured (by design, per DEVOS-037's CI-safety philosophy) — fixed by leaving `AGENT_TASK` simply unregistered (with a clear startup warning) when the key is absent, rather than crashing worker startup entirely; re-verified against the full e2e suite afterward. Full forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79 (Sprint 1's e2e/hardening suites included, confirming both the ordering fix and the regression fix); `pnpm format:check` clean (including the `pnpm-lock.yaml` regenerated by the new `apps/worker` → `@devos/agents` dependency). **Live-verified against real Postgres**, through the actual `TaskDispatcher`/`PostgresTaskQueue` (not manual function calls): started a real run against the seeded 4-node workflow and drove `processNext()` in a loop exactly like a real worker's poll loop. First pass used the real Gemini API and confirmed genuinely correct claim ordering and routing (discovery and requirements both succeeded, in the right order, before the day's cumulative testing exhausted Gemini's free-tier daily quota — confirmed via the actual `429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` responses recorded in `agent_executions`, not a code defect; DEVOS-031–034 already separately live-verified each agent's real-Gemini behavior earlier the same day). A second pass swapped in a fake `AgentModelAdapter` (branching on `configuration.role`) to isolate and cleanly complete verification of DEVOS-035's own actual contribution without further hitting the exhausted quota: all four tasks claimed in the correct order, all succeeded, the run reached `COMPLETED`, and all four artifact types (`DISCOVERY_REPORT`/`PRD`/`TECHNICAL_DESIGN`/`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN`) were produced with correct context-manifest `ARTIFACT` sourcing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

New read-side use case `getAgentExecutionSummariesForRun()` (`packages/application/src/workflows/get-agent-execution-summaries-for-run.ts`, new `AgentExecutionSummaryUseCaseDeps` — a flat sibling of `WorkflowUseCaseDeps`/`ArtifactUseCaseDeps`/etc., not an extension, matching that established precedent) wraps the already-existing `AgentExecutionRepository.listForTask`/`ContextManifestRepository.getForExecution` (DEVOS-026/030) with no new repository code needed. New `GET /api/v1/runs/{runId}/agent-execution-summaries` route (`apps/api/src/routes/agent-execution-summaries.ts`) — **a flagged assumption**: not a literal path in specs/api/poc-api-contracts.md §20 (which documents agent-scoped `/agents/{agentId}/executions`, not this run/task-scoped shape the UI actually needs; no endpoint at all is documented for context manifests, only "via the execution"). Narrowed `getWorkflowRunForPrincipal`'s parameter type from the full `WorkflowUseCaseDeps` to a new minimal `GetWorkflowRunDeps` (just `workflowRuns`+`projects`+`memberships`) so the new use case could reuse it without satisfying unrelated fields — every existing caller still structurally satisfies the narrower type, so this is a non-breaking narrowing, re-confirmed by the full test suite. `RunsPage.tsx` now fetches `listAgentExecutionSummaries(run.id)` alongside its existing run/task polling and renders a `<dl>` per agent task with role, prompt reference, context-manifest source count/types, output (pretty-printed JSON), or error. Full forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean. **Live-verified against real Postgres**: started the real API dev server against the DEVOS-035 verification run's real data and confirmed the endpoint correctly returns all four planning-path stages' role/promptReference/output/context-manifest sources (including the `ARTIFACT`-derived chain from DEVOS-032/033/034), plus correct 401/404 denial for unauthenticated/non-member requests. **UI rendering subsequently confirmed in a real browser**: the user asked that Playwright be installed specifically so this could be self-verified (not part of the project's own dependencies — installed to a scratch npm project, browser binary in the user's local Playwright cache, nothing added to the repo). Drove a real headless Chromium against the real `apps/web`/`apps/api` dev servers and real Postgres (a standalone fake-model-adapter "worker" script stood in for the real Gemini adapter, since the free-tier daily quota was still exhausted — everything else, including the dispatcher/queue/routing/artifact publishing, was real): created a work item, started a run against the seeded planning-path workflow, and confirmed all four stages' Agent/Context manifest/Output panels rendered correctly, with zero browser console errors. **Found and fixed a second real bug via this verification**: `WorkflowTaskRepository.listForRun` (`packages/database/src/repositories/workflow-tasks.ts`) had no `ORDER BY` at all, so the Timeline rendered tasks in an arbitrary order unrelated to execution sequence (first observed as literally "discovery, planning, requirements, technical-design" on screen) — a pre-existing Sprint 1 gap the DEVOS-035 claim-ordering fix didn't touch (that fixed claim order, not display order). Fixed with `.orderBy('created_at', 'asc')`, matching DEVOS-035's per-node offset; re-verified both by re-running the full test suite (79/79) and by re-running the same browser check, which then showed the correct discovery→requirements→technical-design→planning order. This closes DEVOS-036's one open item — the task is now fully COMPLETE, no caveat remaining. |

New `packages/agents/src/fixtures/` convention (mirroring prompts'/schemas' file-based, versioned-reference pattern): `AgentFixture`/`AgentFixtureRepository`/`createFilesystemFixtureRepository()` (`fixture-repository.ts`) and `createFixtureModelAdapter()` (`fixture-model-adapter.ts`, an `AgentModelAdapter` that replays a recorded fixture keyed by role instead of calling a live provider). Four fixture files (`discovery`/`requirements`/`technical-design`/`planning`, each `v1`) populated with **genuinely real Gemini responses** — three carried over from DEVOS-031–034's own live verification earlier the same day, one (`discovery`) freshly re-recorded during this task's own live verification of the refresh script. New `packages/application/tests/agent-fixtures-regression.test.ts` chains all four real `run*AgentTask` handlers against the fixtures, using the real filesystem prompt/schema repositories (only the model call is faked) — proving the recorded fixtures still produce schema-valid, correctly-chained artifacts; this is what would catch drift if a future prompt or schema change breaks compatibility with old recordings. New `packages/agents/src/refresh-fixtures.ts` (`pnpm --filter @devos/agents refresh-fixtures`, requires `GEMINI_API_KEY`) is the scripted refresh path: re-invokes all four stages for real, validates each result against the current schema before writing, chains real output into the next stage's input exactly like production. **Found and fixed a real usability gap in the refresh script itself while live-verifying it**: Gemini's free tier enforces a short rolling-window rate limit (~one request per 30–35s) on top of its daily cap, hit on every real run of this specific tool, not just incidentally — added `invokeWithRateLimitRetry()` (fixed-wait retry, a few attempts) so the script is actually usable in practice rather than failing on the very first real run. Full forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean. **Live-verified against the real Gemini API**: ran the actual refresh script — the discovery stage genuinely succeeded (real call, real schema validation, real file write, confirmed by inspecting the rewritten fixture), and the remaining three stages hit the same free-tier **daily** quota exhaustion already documented for DEVOS-031–035 (a real external constraint, not a script defect) — acceptable since those three fixtures already carry real, valid, schema-conforming recordings from earlier in this same session. | DEVOS-038 — Planning-Path End-to-End                                   | COMPLETE                   | The Sprint 2 analogue of DEVOS-021's proof, and the sprint's own demo scenario (specs/sprints/sprint-02/README.md §10) — the last task in Sprint 2. New `tests/e2e/planning-path.test.ts`, mirroring `vertical-slice.test.ts`'s exact pattern (spawns real API + worker processes via `node`-invoked `tsx`, drives them purely over HTTP, against real Postgres, `pnpm --filter @devos/database run seed` in `beforeAll` matching `hardening.test.ts`'s own precedent). Runs against DEVOS-035's seeded `planning-path` workflow version and its four seeded agents rather than creating fresh ones — a first attempt with freshly-created, differently-keyed agents surfaced a real, previously-latent design constraint in `apps/worker/src/agent-task-router.ts` (DEVOS-035): it resolves a handler by matching the task's `agentRef` against four specific hardcoded seeded keys, not by the resolved agent version's `role`, so any other agent (however correctly configured) has no route today — reusing the purpose-built seeded workflow sidesteps this rather than force a router redesign mid-DEVOS-038; flagged as a real limitation worth revisiting if a future task needs dynamically-created agents to route correctly. New `AGENT_MODEL_ADAPTER=fixture` env toggle in `apps/worker/src/main.ts` (async `resolveAgentModelAdapter()`, using top-level `await` — swaps in DEVOS-037's recorded golden fixtures instead of a real Gemini call) is what lets this test spawn the **exact real worker binary** and still pass repeatably in CI with zero live API calls and no `GEMINI_API_KEY`, matching DEVOS-016/037's established "unit tests against fakes/fixtures, live calls reserved for deliberate verification" philosophy — not set in normal/production use, so this is a pure test-support addition, unused otherwise. Audit assertions are scoped to this run's own `targetId`s (run id, each artifact id) rather than the whole shared seeded project's audit feed, since that project accumulates every manual verification session run against it. Full forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79 (all three `@devos/e2e-tests` files together, confirming no interference between them); `pnpm format:check` clean; confirmed no leftover API/worker processes after the run, matching DEVOS-021's own diligence standard. **Sprint 2 (DEVOS-025–038) is COMPLETE.** New `authority.ts` maps each source 's `type` to a conceptual authority level (§18) — an explicit implementation choice since the spec defers concrete precedence to a nonexistent later document; `ARTIFACT` is deliberately ranked low (Generated/Proposed, not yet Approved) since planning artifacts are not human-approved until DEVOS-047. Sources are sorted highest-authority-first, then capped by `maxSources`/`maxContentLength`, dropping lowest-authority sources first (never truncating one source's content). | DEVOS-044 — Implement Policy Evaluator                                 | COMPLETE                   | New `packages/policy/src/evaluator/` (the package's first real code) — `evaluatePolicies()`, a deterministic, hand-rolled evaluator: a minimal, implementation-defined rule shape for `Policy.definition` (`{rules: [{action, effect, condition?}], defaultEffect?}`), since no evaluation algorithm is specified anywhere in the spec corpus. Only the highest-version PUBLISHED row per key governs (DRAFT policies are ignored entirely); precedence is specific-rule-match > policy defaultEffect > a global ALLOW fallback (explicitly flagged: this POC's workflow already enforces its own hard-coded gates independent of policy, so an unaddressed action is not silently DENY-blocked). Lives in `packages/policy`, not `packages/domain` (the entity) — the evaluation algorithm is exactly the responsibility the repo-structure spec assigns that package. Not yet wired into any real decision point (DEVOS-047 is the first real consumer). New unit tests (7 cases: rule match, condition match/non-match, policy defaultEffect fallback, global fallback, DRAFT ignored, version precedence, determinism). **Live-verified against real Postgres**: created and published a real policy through the real `PolicyRepository`, fetched it back, and ran the evaluator against the real JSONB-round-tripped definition — confirmed correct `REQUIRE_APPROVAL`/`ALLOW`/`DENY` outcomes. | DEVOS-051 — Implement Tool Definition Model                            | COMPLETE                   | New `tool_capabilities` table (migration `0022`) matching `specs/database/poc-database-schema.md` §14.1 exactly. New `ToolCapability`/`ToolCapabilityRepository` (`packages/domain/src/tools/`), `ToolCapabilityId`/`ToolCapabilityRiskClass` (R0–R4)/`ToolCapabilityStatus` (ACTIVE/DISABLED) contract types. No `tool_permissions` table exists anywhere in the documented schema despite domain-model §11.3 defining one — flagged rather than fabricated; DEVOS-052 binds authority via DEVOS-044's policy evaluator instead. CRUD in `packages/application/src/tools/` (`registerCapability` is get-or-create, not create-or-fail, mirroring the seed script's idempotent pattern). `packages/tools/src/capabilities/` holds the canonical definitions for this sprint's four capabilities (`repo-read` R0, `repo-write`/`git-commit` R2, `pull-request-create` R3, per the Risk Model table); `issue-read`/`issue-update`/`build-run`/`test-run`/`deploy` deliberately not created (later sprints' scope). `packages/tools/src/registry/` exposes lookup + a `registerAllCapabilities` helper; the database seed script inlines the same four rows as literal constants (packages/database cannot depend on packages/tools/application). New unit tests (8 in `@devos/application`, 3 in `@devos/tools`). **Live-verified against real Postgres**: ran the migration, ran `pnpm db:seed`, queried `tool_capabilities` directly confirming all 4 rows with correct risk classes/JSONB schemas; re-ran the seed a second time confirming idempotency (still 4 rows). Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 78/79 on the first pass — the one failure (`@devos/e2e-tests`) was the same transient `beforeAll` hook-timeout flake seen repeatedly in Sprint 3, confirmed transient via a standalone re-run (5/5) and a second full forced re-validation (79/79 clean). `pnpm format:check` clean except a pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning confirmed (via `git stash`) to already exist on `main` before this task. | DEVOS-052 — Implement Tool Gateway                                     | COMPLETE                   | New `tool_invocations` table (migration `0023`) matching `specs/database/poc-database-schema.md` §14.2 exactly. New `ToolInvocation`/`ToolInvocationRepository` and `ToolInvocationStatus` (REJECTED/SUCCEEDED/FAILED, PROVISIONAL). `invokeTool()` (`packages/tools/src/gateway/`) implements the full chain from `poc-api-contracts.md` §56: Typed Validation -> Project Scope -> Policy -> Capability Permission -> Credential Resolution -> Provider Adapter. One reconciliation logged: capability lookup happens right after Project Scope (ahead of the named 'Capability Permission' step) because `tool_invocations.tool_capability_id` is a required FK — no row, including a Policy rejection, can be recorded without it; Policy still evaluates using the capability's own key as the action, exactly as named. DEVOS-044's policy evaluator is now consulted for real, closing the gap DEVOS-SPRINT3-DECISIONS.md flagged. Credential Resolution is a deliberate no-op (DEVOS-053 not built yet); Provider Adapter is an injected per-capability-key map, empty until DEVOS-054/058 register real adapters — invoking a capability with none registered is itself a valid recorded FAILED outcome (DEVOS_NO_PROVIDER_ADAPTER). New unit tests (8 cases: success/no-adapter/adapter-throws/disabled/policy-deny/invalid-input/unknown-capability/non-member). **Live-verified against real Postgres**: ran the migration; created an isolated verification project after discovering a leftover DEVOS-044 verification policy's `defaultEffect: DENY` was leaking into the shared seeded project (pre-existing verification-hygiene debt, not a bug in this task); confirmed all four real outcomes (SUCCEEDED with a real adapter invoked, REJECTED on invalid input, FAILED with no adapter, REJECTED against a real published DENY policy). Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. Reconciliation logged: §13.1 has no `credential_reference` column despite `poc-api-contracts.md` §33 requiring one — nested inside the `configuration` JSONB instead of an undocumented column, since a reference name is not itself secret material; the domain entity/CRUD still expose it as a first-class field, only the repository knows it's nested at the row level. `CredentialResolver` (`packages/integrations/src/credential-resolver.ts`) resolves a reference from `process.env` by name — a generalization of the existing GEMINI_API_KEY-via-@devos/config precedent, not a secrets-manager integration, exactly as the task's own spec anticipates. | DEVOS-054 — Implement Git Adapter                                      | COMPLETE                   | New `packages/integrations/src/github/` following the exact adapter-internal structure (`github.client.ts`/`github.mapper.ts`/`github.schemas.ts`/`github.adapter.ts`) `specs/architecture/repository-code-structure.md` §36 names. No concrete GitHub API contract, and no `repositories`/`branches`/`commits` table/domain entity, exists anywhere — flagged. Git itself is provider-agnostic, so `openRepository`/`cloneRepository`/`createBranch`/`writeFileChange`/`createCommit` are implemented purely against the real `git` CLI (via `execFile`, never `shell: true`) with zero GitHub-specific code; only PR creation (DEVOS-058) will be GitHub-API-specific. This task's acceptance criterion is unusual: 'verified against a real local git repository (not a mock of the git CLI)' — since there's no fake client to bridge from, the automated test suite itself is the live verification (a real throwaway repo via `mkdtemp`+`git init` in `beforeEach`, exercised through every function, torn down after). Found (not a bug): Windows Git's `core.autocrlf=true` rewrites LF->CRLF on checkout — the clone test normalizes line endings rather than asserting an exact byte match. Test fixture sets local (not global) `user.email`/`user.name` after `git init` so commits succeed regardless of the machine's global git config. No wiring into `packages/tools`'s `ProviderAdapter` interface yet — deferred to whichever later task (likely DEVOS-057) actually composes it, avoiding an unneeded `integrations` -> `tools` coupling. New unit tests (6 cases, all against a real local git repo). Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. | DEVOS-055 — Implement Controlled Workspace                             | COMPLETE                   | Thinnest task in the sprint, by its own spec: 'workspace' appears in the spec corpus exactly once, a negative constraint ('Development agent workspaces must not contain unrelated credentials,' repository-code-structure.md §47) — no entity/table/lifecycle/isolation mechanism specified. `packages/integrations/src/workspace/development-workspace.ts` implements the flagged assumption: a temporary local directory, one per development task (keyed by WorkflowTaskId), populated via DEVOS-054's `cloneRepository`, removed afterward — mirroring the existing mkdtemp/rm temp-directory pattern rather than container-based isolation. 'No unrelated credentials' is satisfied by construction (createWorkspace takes no CredentialResolver, reads no secrets). 'Reliably cleaned up whether the task succeeds or fails' is enforced mechanically via a `withWorkspace` try/finally helper, not left as a convention. Added `@devos/contracts` as a new (lightweight, no-cycle) dependency of packages/integrations for WorkflowTaskId. New unit tests (5 cases), all against a real local git repository and real filesystem — this task's own test suite is its live verification, same as DEVOS-054. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. | DEVOS-056 — Implement Repository Context                               | COMPLETE                   | Repository Context as a category is well-specified (system-context-engineering-knowledge.md §10), but the retrieval mechanism is explicitly deferred (§39: 'does not decide... repository indexing technology; file chunking strategy; retrieval algorithms'). New `listRepositoryFiles`/`retrieveRepositoryListing`/`retrieveRepositoryFile`/`searchRepository` in `packages/knowledge/src/retrieval/` — a bounded filesystem walk plus plain substring search, explicitly not vector search/embeddings. Placed in packages/knowledge (not packages/integrations) to keep the existing RetrievedSource family in one place and avoid an undocumented knowledge->integrations dependency; every function takes a plain repositoryPath/revision string rather than a DevelopmentWorkspace, keeping packages/knowledge free of any git dependency (obtaining the revision is the caller's job). Bounded at two levels: file-count cap (default 200, .git/node_modules/dist/.turbo excluded) and per-file byte cap (default 100k, marks truncated:true rather than silently returning a partial file). New unit tests (6 cases, real temp directory tree, no mocked filesystem). **Live-verified end-to-end**: a scratch script composed a real DEVOS-055 workspace (cloning a real local git repo) with these retrieval functions, confirming real cloned files, a RetrievedSource tagged with the real commit SHA, real byte-for-byte file content, and a real substring match — proving the two packages compose correctly despite no compile-time dependency between them. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. | DEVOS-057 — Implement Development Agent                                | COMPLETE                   | Stage 7 - Development: new `development-agent` Agent/AgentVersion (role DEVELOPMENT), a normal agent definition like DEVOS-031-034, not a new domain entity. New prompt (developer/v1) and schema (proposed-change-v1: summary/branchName/commitMessage/files). Per Constitution Principle 6/7, the model never writes to the repository — `runDevelopmentAgentTask` only gets a schema-validated proposed change back from `runAgentTask`; deterministic platform code then applies it through the Tool Gateway. `createGitProviderAdapters` wires DEVOS-054's Git adapter into DEVOS-052's ProviderAdapter shape, closing the gap DEVOS-054 deferred here. A DEVOS-055 workspace is created per task and destroyed in a finally block regardless of outcome. **Found and fixed: a real circular dependency** (`packages/tools` had depended on `@devos/application` since DEVOS-051, already backwards relative to the documented direction; this task's own correctly-directed `application -> tools` dependency turned it into an actual cycle turbo refused to build) — fixed by moving the shared error classes into `packages/domain` (both packages already depend on it) and relocating `registerAllCapabilities` from `packages/tools` into `packages/application` (it needs application's CRUD; tools no longer depends on application at all). **Found and fixed: a workspace-lifetime bug** — a commit made inside an ephemeral DEVOS-055 workspace was silently discarded once the workspace was destroyed; added `pushBranch` to the Git adapter (cloning from a local path auto-configures `origin` as that path, so pushing back is itself a local operation) and wired it into the git-commit ProviderAdapter. New unit tests (2 in @devos/application for the handler, plus the relocated registerAllCapabilities case). **Live-verified with a real Gemini call**: a one-off script made one real call for the developer/v1 prompt, passed schema validation, and was recorded as a fixture; a second script then ran the full handler against real Postgres + a real throwaway git repo + that fixture, confirming 2 real tool_invocations rows, a real commit pushed and visible via git log/show, and a real CODE_CHANGE artifact published — after first hitting (and fixing, via an isolated verification project) the same cross-task pollution pattern DEVOS-052 found. Not yet registered in apps/worker's dispatch table or a seeded workflow — deferred to DEVOS-061. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79, no circular-dependency warning; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. | DEVOS-058 — Implement PR Creation Capability                           | COMPLETE                   | New `PullRequestProvider` port (`packages/integrations/src/pull-requests/`) and its fake/local implementation, `createLocalPullRequestProvider()` — the only implementation built this sprint (user-authorized scoping decision). No concrete GitHub PR schema/endpoint/auth exists anywhere in the spec corpus, flagged. Reuses DEVOS-051's already-registered `pull-request-create` key (hyphenated) rather than the dotted `git.pull_request.create` this task's own spec quotes from `poc-api-contracts.md` §32 — introducing a second capability for the same operation would fragment the model; the naming mismatch is flagged, not silently resolved. `createPullRequestProviderAdapter` wires the provider into DEVOS-052's ProviderAdapter shape, mirroring DEVOS-057's git adapter wiring; since ProviderAdapter.invoke doesn't receive the invocation's own idempotencyKey, the caller duplicates it into `parameters.idempotencyKey`. Idempotency is implemented at the provider (in-memory map keyed by idempotencyKey), deliberately narrower than DEVOS-059's later gateway-level mutation-safety controls — two invokeTool calls with the same key today still record two tool_invocations rows, both pointing at the same PR. New unit tests (3 in @devos/integrations, 2 in @devos/application). Found and fixed a real bug: the fake provider's `url` used an independent randomUUID() instead of reusing `id`. **Live-verified against real Postgres + real git, chaining directly off DEVOS-054/057's machinery**: a real branch with two real commits, registerAllCapabilities for the project, two invokeTool calls with the same idempotency key — both SUCCEEDED with the same providerReference, two real tool_invocations rows recorded. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. | DEVOS-059 — Add Mutation Safety Controls                               | COMPLETE                   | Neither 'branch binding' nor 'mutation safety' is a named mechanism anywhere in the spec corpus (flagged); idempotency itself is well-grounded. New `idempotency_key` column on tool_invocations (migration `0025`) plus `getByCapabilityAndIdempotencyKey` — deliberately NO unique constraint (unlike workflow_runs' equivalent, DEVOS-014), since a branch-binding-violation row must persist sharing the same (capability, key) pair; real dedup is enforced in invokeTool itself, not the database. invokeTool now looks up an existing invocation by (capability, idempotencyKey) right after capability resolution: exact target/parameters match -> return the original row unchanged (genuine replay, no new row, no second adapter call, still audited); mismatch -> new REJECTED row, DEVOS_TOOL_BRANCH_BINDING_VIOLATION, audited FAILURE. New `auditRecords: AuditRecordRepository` added to ToolGatewayDeps (from @devos/domain, no new package dependency) — every exit path now writes exactly one audit record via a shared recordAndAudit helper. Updated every existing in-memory ToolGatewayDeps/DevelopmentAgentTaskHandlerDeps test fixture across 3 files for the new shape; corrected (not just updated) one DEVOS-058 test assertion whose own comment described exactly the gap this task closes. New unit tests (2 cases + an audit assertion on the existing success case). **Live-verified against real Postgres**: a real invocation SUCCEEDED and was recorded; an exact replay returned the identical row; a same-key-different-branch replay was REJECTED as a genuinely new row sharing the same idempotency_key (proving the no-unique-constraint decision was necessary); exactly 3 real audit_records rows for 3 invokeTool calls, including the replay. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. Marked COMPLETE. No separate workspace/diff view built — no spec anchor, not required by the acceptance criterion. Found and cleaned up two leftover dev-server child processes that survived stopping the parent background task (a Windows/turbo process-tree quirk). | DEVOS-061 — Development E2E                                            | COMPLETE                   | Wires the DEVOS-057 development agent into `apps/worker`'s dispatch table and a real seeded `development-path` workflow (a genuinely separate workflow/run, not a fifth `planning-path` node — an Explore investigation of `maybeCompleteRun`/`run-creation.ts`/`createApprovalRunTransition` found the current engine only advances a run's approval gate once _every_ task in it succeeds, so co-locating development with planning would block planning-approval on development also completing). Plan lookup changed from run-scoped to project-scoped, since a development-path run has no relationship to the planning-path run that produced its plan beyond sharing a project. PR creation folded into the same task (Stage 7's own 'expected activities' list includes it), not a separate task/node. `apps/worker/src/main.ts` wired to the full `DevelopmentAgentTaskHandlerDeps` shape with real repositories plus `createLocalPullRequestProvider()`, and gained a `DEVELOPMENT`-role fixture mapping so the real worker binary can run this stage with `AGENT_MODEL_ADAPTER=fixture`. **Three real bugs found during live verification, all fixed**: (1) `Artifact.createdAt` is typed `string` but pg returns `timestamptz` columns as `Date` at runtime — `.localeCompare` crashed; fixed locally in this task's own new sort with a `Date`-safe comparison, flagged as pre-existing repo-wide debt outside this task's scope. (2) A third instance of this sprint's recurring cross-task verification-data pollution: a DEVOS-044 live-verification policy (`devos044-verify-release-approval`, `defaultEffect: DENY`, no rule addressing repo-write/git-commit/pull-request-create) silently denied all three capabilities project-wide — unlike the first two occurrences, a fresh isolated project wasn't an option here (the seeded workflows are fixed to `SEED_PROJECT_ID`), so the new E2E test's `beforeAll` un-publishes that one specific stray policy instead. (3) `invokeTool`'s idempotency-replay comparison (DEVOS-059) was key-order-sensitive — Postgres `jsonb` storage doesn't preserve object key order, so a genuine identical retry was rejected as a `DEVOS_TOOL_BRANCH_BINDING_VIOLATION`; fixed with a `stableStringify` helper (sorts keys before comparing), with a new regression test. New E2E test `tests/e2e/development-path.test.ts` (port 3903): real planning run to an approved plan, then a real separate development-path run consuming it, asserting `COMPLETED` status, all three tool invocations `SUCCEEDED`, `commitSha`/`pullRequestReference` on the `CODE_CHANGE` artifact's metadata, and audit records for every tool invocation. **Live-verified**: the full `tests/e2e` suite (4 files, 6 tests) passes together against real Postgres and real spawned processes. Full forced (no-cache) `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force` at 79/79; `pnpm format:check` clean except the same pre-existing `pnpm-lock.yaml` warning. No real GitHub API call was made anywhere in Sprint 4 (verification debt carried forward by design, per the sprint's user-authorized scoping decision). Marked COMPLETE. **Sprint 4 (DEVOS-051–061) is COMPLETE** — pausing per the same governance Sprints 1–3's completion followed, awaiting the user's explicit direction before Sprint 5.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

New `ToolTaskHandlerDeps` (does not extend any agent-runtime deps shape). Consumes the project's latest `CODE_CHANGE` artifact (project-scoped), checks out its recorded `branchName` in a fresh DEVOS-055 workspace, resolves build/test commands from the Git integration's own configuration (new optional `buildCommand`/`testCommand` fields, not a new Integration type). A non-zero exit code does not fail the task — it's recorded as `passed: false` data on the published `TEST_EVIDENCE` artifact, not a task failure; only a genuine infrastructure problem throws. New unit tests: `packages/application/tests/run-validation-task.test.ts` (3 cases against a real local git repository and real `node -e` commands). Not yet wired into `apps/worker` (no seeded workflow node yet) — deferred until the review agent (DEVOS-065/066) exists too, mirroring DEVOS-054-056's supporting-piece-first sequencing. Full package-scoped validation clean. Marked COMPLETE. First real wiring of DEVOS-040's `retrieveActiveKnowledgeSources` into a live task handler (closing a verification-debt item) for 'engineering standards'. The model's `decision` is normalized defensively -- only literal `PASS` counts as a pass, anything else becomes `CHANGES_REQUIRED` (the generic schema validator has no enum support, confirmed by reading it). New REVIEW_EVIDENCE artifact type. New unit tests (4 cases, no filesystem needed). **Live-verified with a real Gemini call**: one-off script recorded a real `review/v1` fixture, schema-validated on the first try. | DEVOS-067 — Rework Transitions, DEVOS-068 — Retry/Rework Limits        | COMPLETE                   | Seeded a v2 development-path WorkflowVersion (not an edit to v1, which stays immutable per Workflow Principle 8) with 3 nodes: development → validation (TOOL_TASK) → review (AGENT_TASK) — no human gate between them so the existing engine model already fits within one run. A CHANGES_REQUIRED decision still can’t loop _within_ a run (same all-tasks-must-succeed constraint DEVOS-061 found), so `runReviewAgentTask` calls `startRunForVersion` directly to start a new run of the same version for the same work item. Rework count lives on `workItem.metadata.reworkCount` (simpler/more precise than scanning artifacts, which have no work-item link of their own); `MAX_AUTOMATIC_REWORK_CYCLES = 2` (flagged assumption, no spec bound exists) — at the limit, `workItem.status` becomes `REWORK_LIMIT_REACHED` instead of looping. Enhanced `runDevelopmentAgentTask` to fold the latest CHANGES_REQUIRED review’s findings into the model input as `priorReviewFindings`, satisfying "the developer/agent receives the relevant findings" (§20). **Found and fixed a real bug**: a rework’s fresh workspace reusing the same branch name would fail to push (non-fast-forward) since `createBranch` always branched from the default branch’s HEAD; fixed to resume an existing remote branch when one exists. Wired `apps/worker`: `TOOL_TASK` → `runValidationTask`, `review-agent` added to `routeAgentTask`, REVIEW-role fixture mapping. New unit tests (3 in run-review-agent-task.test.ts, 1 in run-development-agent-task.test.ts, 1 real-git regression test in github-adapter.test.ts). Full package-scoped validation clean. Marked COMPLETE. New `NonRetryableTaskError` (`@devos/domain`) is an opt-in signal the dispatcher now checks for; every other handler is unaffected. `ToolInvocation.status`'s existing `REJECTED`/`FAILED` split maps directly onto Retry Rules 1/2 — a new `requireSucceeded()` helper throws `NonRetryableTaskError` for a policy-denied (`REJECTED`) deploy/health-check and a plain (retryable) `Error` for a genuine (`FAILED`) one. Rollback reuses `runReleaseTask`'s exact deploy/health-check/evidence pipeline via a shared `performRelease()` helper — new `runReleaseRollbackTask` requires an explicit `rollbackToRevision` with no automatic/latest fallback, so rollback can never be silently self-triggered. | DEVOS-083 — Secret management integration                              | COMPLETE                   | Audited the existing reference-only credential mechanism (DEVOS-053's `CredentialResolver`/`createEnvCredentialResolver`) end-to-end: confirmed by inspection that no production code path currently calls `.resolve()` at all (it remains an unwired, tested-in-isolation utility — no adapter needs a live secret while real GitHub/external-deployment calls stay out of scope per the carried-forward Sprint 4-6 decision, so wiring a consumer now would mean prematurely building a real external provider, which AGENTS.md §21 forbids); confirmed `apps/api` has no integrations route at all yet, so there is no HTTP response surface to leak through; confirmed the global error handler already returns a generic message for unexpected errors and never echoes request bodies; confirmed no request-body logging middleware exists. Found one real, concrete gap and fixed it: `configuration` (a free-form JSONB bag) had no schema, so a caller could paste an actual secret value into it (e.g. `configuration.token`) instead of going through `credentialReference`, defeating the reference-only guarantee for that field. Added `assertNoSecretShapedConfiguration` to `create-integration.ts`, recursively rejecting configuration keys matching a secret-like pattern (`password`, `secret`, `token`, `api[-_]?key`, `credential`). 4 new unit tests (2 rejection cases — top-level and nested — plus 2 confirming ordinary configuration still passes). `CredentialResolver` remaining unwired is recorded as accepted, explained debt, not silently dropped. | DEVOS-085 — Agent/tool security controls                               | COMPLETE                   | Closed the pre-verified gap: `AgentVersion.configuration.allowedCapabilities` was validated on input and stored (DEVOS-025) but never checked anywhere at runtime. Added a new, optional `agentVersionId` field to `InvokeToolInput`; when supplied, `invokeTool` (`packages/tools/src/gateway/invoke-tool.ts`) now looks the version up via a new optional `ToolGatewayDeps.agentVersions` and rejects with `DEVOS_AGENT_CAPABILITY_DENIED` (recorded + audited like every other gateway rejection) unless the capability key is present in that version's `allowedCapabilities` — both fields optional so every existing caller without an agent behind the invocation (build/test/deploy task handlers) is unaffected. Updated the seeded development agent's `allowedCapabilities` from `[]` to the three keys it actually needs (previously vestigial; now load-bearing). New tests: 3 focused gateway-level tests in `packages/tools/tests/invoke-tool.test.ts` (denies an excluded capability, allows an included one, denies an unresolvable agent version id) plus 1 full-stack escalation test in `run-development-agent-task.test.ts` (an agent version missing `repo-write` is rejected before the git adapter is ever reached — no branch created). **Live-verification caught a real self-inflicted regression**: the persisted Postgres seed data (fixed-UUID, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` upsert) still held the pre-change `allowedCapabilities: []` from the volume's prior state, so the full forced e2e suite initially failed — not a code defect, but a real gap between "changed the seed script" and "the already-seeded database reflects it," fixed with a direct one-row `UPDATE` matching the new seed value, then reconfirmed with a second full run. Also fixed two pre-existing, unrelated flaky-timeout tests hit during forced parallel validation (`local-staging-deployment-provider.test.ts`, `run-release-task.test.ts`), the same recurring 30s-timeout pattern from Sprints 5-6. | DEVOS-086 — Implement structured audit                                 | COMPLETE                   | Confirmed by grep before implementing: `writeAuditRecord` was only ever called from workflow-run lifecycle, artifact publish, context manifest recording, approval decisions, and work-item closure — membership add/remove/role-change, policy publish, integration creation, and agent-version publish wrote no audit record at all. Scoped to exactly these four operation families (the same explicit scoping decision recorded in this task's own spec file), not every CRUD operation in the codebase. Extended `ProjectUseCaseDeps`, `IntegrationUseCaseDeps`, `PolicyUseCaseDeps`, `AgentUseCaseDeps` with a required `auditRecords: AuditRecordRepository`; each of the four use cases (`add-member.ts`, `remove-member.ts`, `change-member-role.ts`, `create-integration.ts`, `publish-policy.ts`, `publish-agent-version.ts`) now writes a real audit record (`membership.added`/`membership.removed`/`membership.role_changed`/`integration.created`/`policy.published`/`agent_version.published`) immediately after its state change succeeds. Discovered mid-task that `resolveMembership`/`assertNotLastOwner` (`membership-access.ts`) were typed to accept the _entire_ `ProjectUseCaseDeps`, even though they only ever touch `memberships` — meaning ~20 unrelated use-case deps interfaces across the whole `packages/application` tree would have been forced to also carry `auditRecords` just to satisfy that structural type. Fixed by narrowing both functions (and the pre-existing `GetWorkflowRunDeps`/`GetWorkflowRunsForWorkItemDeps`, which had the same over-broad `extends ProjectUseCaseDeps`) to a new minimal `MembershipAccessDeps` interface — a real, useful tightening this task's own change surfaced, not scope creep. New unit tests: 1 per operation family (4 total) asserting the audit trail contains the expected `action`/`targetType`/`targetId`/`outcome`, plus one specifically confirming the integration-creation audit record never contains the credential reference value itself. `apps/api/src/app.ts` wired one shared real `AuditRecordRepository` instance across `projectDeps`/`auditDeps`/`agentDeps`/`policyDeps`. New `createMetricsRegistry()`: a real, in-process counter+histogram registry, labeled series keyed by name+sorted-label-values, with its own `snapshot()`/`getCounter()`/`getHistogram()` query interface — no external metrics backend (Prometheus, etc.), consistent with the sprint's own exclusion. Fully optional (`metrics?:`) so every existing caller/test is unaffected. Wired for real in `apps/worker/src/main.ts` (`export const metrics = createMetricsRegistry()`, passed into the dispatcher). New tests: 9 in `packages/observability/tests/registry.test.ts` (counters, label-order independence, histograms, snapshot, registry isolation) and 6 new in `apps/worker/tests/task-dispatcher.test.ts` (claim+completion, retrying vs failed classification by error type, no-handler failure, real non-zero reclaim count, and confirming the dispatcher works identically with no metrics supplied at all). | DEVOS-088 — Implement tracing                                          | COMPLETE                   | Confirmed by inspection that `apps/api/src/app.ts` already resolves a correlation id per request (`resolveCorrelationId`, echoed as `x-correlation-id`/`meta.requestId`) but never passed it to any route handler, so it never reached a workflow run or the agent/tool activity it causes — the exact gap this task closes. `RouteContext` (`apps/api/src/http/router.ts`) gained a required `correlationId: string`, threaded from `handleRequest`. `StartRunInput` (`run-creation.ts`) gained an optional `correlationId?: string`, folded into the run's own `input` and every one of its tasks' `input` under a reserved key — the same extensibility point `node.agentRef` already established, not a new database column. `InvokeToolInput` (`packages/tools`) gained an optional `correlationId?: string`, recorded in `inputMetadata` and in the resulting audit record's own (pre-existing) `correlationId` field. Wired at the three real task handlers that call `invokeTool` (`run-development-agent-task.ts`, `run-validation-task.ts`, `run-release-task.ts`), each reading `task.input.correlationId` and passing it through. No real distributed-tracing backend (OpenTelemetry, Jaeger) — correlation-id propagation and structured-record correlation only, matching the sprint's own exclusion. New tests: 1 in `packages/application/tests/workflows.test.ts` (a supplied correlationId appears in both the run's and its task's stored `input`), 2 in `packages/tools/tests/invoke-tool.test.ts` (present vs. absent, both correctly reflected in `inputMetadata` and the audit record), 1 in `apps/api/tests/app.test.ts` (a real HTTP request supplying `x-correlation-id` results in the exact same id echoed in the response and stored on the created run, verified through the real route). No real Gemini API call was made for this specific task (`GEMINI_API_KEY` not set in this session) — usage-parsing is grounded in Gemini's own long-stable, publicly documented `usageMetadata` response contract, honestly flagged as not freshly re-verified live, unlike Sprint 2/4's own fixture-recording tasks. | DEVOS-091 — Security review                                            | COMPLETE                   | An audit/analysis task, not a feature-build task: a structured walkthrough of every real trust boundary (API->workflow, workflow->agent, agent->tool, tool->external provider/shell) as they stand after DEVOS-082-090, not a formal STRIDE exercise or external pen test (neither is in scope). Confirmed structurally sound: Constitution Principle 6 holds (every real `invokeTool` call site passes a hardcoded `SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID`, never anything derived from model output); `runGit` uses `execFile` with an argv array (immune to shell injection); the Gemini API key is only ever sent via a header, never logged. **One real, previously-unaddressed gap found and fixed**: `specs/api/poc-api-contracts.md` §41 requires rate-limiting expensive endpoints; none existed anywhere in `apps/api`. New real, in-process sliding-window `RateLimiter` (`apps/api/src/http/rate-limiter.ts`), applied to every mutating (POST/PATCH/DELETE) request, keyed per principal, returning a real `429 DEVOS_RATE_LIMITED`; overridable via `CreateAppOptions.mutationRateLimiter` for deterministic testing. **A genuinely retrospective finding surfaced by this review**: before this same sprint's DEVOS-082, `createIntegration` had no RBAC check at all, meaning any project MEMBER could set an arbitrary `buildCommand`/`testCommand`/`healthCheckCommand` later executed by `run-command.ts`'s real shell (`child_process.exec`) — a real command-injection-adjacent privilege-escalation path DEVOS-082's OWNER-gating fix (not originally framed this way) already closed; confirmed still correctly gated. Six further items were reviewed and classified accepted risk with documented reasoning (unwired `CredentialResolver`; agent-capability enforcement scoped to one call site; `createPolicy`/`requestApproval`/`registerCapability` allowing MEMBER-level draft/pending creation while the actual privilege boundary stays OWNER-gated; DEVOS-086's 4-family audit scope; the local dev auth provider's bearer-token-as-principal-id design; unconditional CORS origin reflection, safe since no cookies/credentials are used). New tests: 3 in `rate-limiter.test.ts`, 1 real-429 case in `app.test.ts`. Full monorepo `pnpm turbo run typecheck test lint build --force`: 79/79 tasks green, including the full real `tests/e2e` suite (10/10), confirming the new rate limiter's generous budget doesn't interfere with real workflow execution. `pnpm format:check` clean. | DEVOS-092 — Operational recovery tests                                 | COMPLETE                   | Extends DEVOS-024's own hardening coverage (duplicate-claim safety, stale-task reclaim, max-attempts exhaustion) with two genuinely new scenarios, using real `TaskDispatcher` instances (`@devos/worker`, newly given a proper package entry point — `src/index.ts` + `main`/`types` in `package.json` — since nothing had ever imported its internals across a package boundary before; `main.ts`, the real process entrypoint, is unaffected). **Scenario 1**: a worker that crashes mid-task (a handler that never resolves, calling neither `complete()` nor `fail()` — indistinguishable from a real process death from the queue's own perspective) has its task recovered and finished by a second, fully independent dispatcher instance sharing the same real Postgres queue, through each dispatcher's own real, timer-driven reclaim loop — not a manually-invoked `reclaimStale()` call the way DEVOS-024's own test exercises the primitive in isolation. **Scenario 2, a real bug found and fixed**: `apps/worker/src/task-dispatcher.ts`'s `loop()` had no error handling around its own `queue.claimNext()`/`reclaimStale()` calls — confirmed by temporarily reverting the fix and re-running the new tests, which failed exactly as predicted with an unhandled promise rejection: a single transient queue-level failure (e.g. a dropped DB connection) would silently and permanently kill the entire dispatch loop, with nothing watching the unawaited `loopPromise` to ever notice or recover. Fixed by wrapping each loop iteration in a try/catch that logs a new `task_queue.dispatch_error` metric and retries after the normal poll interval — the same minimal treatment already given to "nothing to claim." New tests: 2 unit tests in `apps/worker/tests/task-dispatcher.test.ts` (transient `claimNext()`/`reclaimStale()` failures, both proven to fail before the fix and pass after) and 2 real, live-Postgres e2e tests in `tests/e2e/hardening.test.ts` (the crash-and-recover scenario; a transient-failure scenario using a real Postgres-backed queue wrapped to inject one failure). | DEVOS-094 — Reliability hardening                                      | COMPLETE                   | `TaskQueue.complete()`/`fail()` previously updated a row by id alone; a worker genuinely still working past `reclaimStale()`'s time-based threshold could be reclaimed while alive, and its later real completion would silently overwrite the reclaiming worker's outcome. Fixed at the `TaskQueue` interface level (`packages/domain/src/workflows/task-queue.ts`): `complete()`/`fail()` now take the caller's believed `attempt` as a fencing token; the real Postgres implementation (`packages/database/src/repositories/task-queue.ts`) applies the UPDATE only `WHERE status = 'RUNNING' AND attempt = attempt` via `executeTakeFirst()` — no match is a safe no-op, not corruption. `apps/worker/src/task-dispatcher.ts` threads `task.attempt` through all three `complete()`/`fail()` call sites. New real-Postgres test in `tests/e2e/hardening.test.ts` reproduces the exact race (worker A claims, is reclaimed while still "working," worker B completes first, A's late completion is confirmed a true no-op). **Real bug proven, not just asserted**: fix reverted, `@devos/database` rebuilt (the e2e suite imports its compiled `dist/`), test re-run and failed exactly as predicted (`workerA-late` clobbered `workerB`'s recorded output); fix restored and reconfirmed. Forced `pnpm turbo run typecheck test lint build --filter=@devos/domain --filter=@devos/database --filter=@devos/worker --force`: 22/22 green. `@devos/e2e-tests` typecheck/lint clean; real-Postgres suite 13/13 green (6/6 files). `prettier --check` clean. (2) DEVOS-096's task-dispatch-ordering race, root-caused (not fixed) by DEVOS-108 — now genuinely fixed: `run-creation.ts` folds each node's real upstream dependencies (from the workflow graph's own declared edges) into its task's `input.dependsOn`; `claimNext()` (`packages/database/src/repositories/task-queue.ts`) now refuses to claim a task until every dependency named there has reached `SUCCEEDED` in the same run, enforced atomically inside the same claiming query (this codebase's first use of Kysely's raw `sql` tag). Verified first in isolation (a hand-built SQL reproduction against a temp table, no application code), then end-to-end against real Postgres (`tests/e2e/hardening.test.ts`, 3 new tests, one deliberately giving the downstream task an _earlier_ `createdAt` than its upstream to prove the barrier is real). A companion fix: a permanently-failed task now marks its still-`PENDING` siblings `FAILED` too (`DEVOS_UPSTREAM_TASK_FAILED`), preserving "a failed run's tasks are all terminal" rather than leaving them claimable-but-never-claimed forever. Hit the same dist-vs-source staleness lesson DEVOS-108 itself already flagged once, a second time (`tests/e2e` imports `@devos/database`'s compiled `dist/`) — a rebuild resolved it. A separate ~47-minute anomalous full-suite run was investigated (not dismissed) and attributed to this session's own concurrent heavy activity, not a livelock in the fix, based on `pg_stat_activity` showing no blocking and three other full-suite runs completing cleanly in 58-71s. **Full validation:** `@devos/database`+`@devos/application` typecheck/test: 12/12 tasks green (193 total application tests, +1 new). `tests/e2e/hardening.test.ts` alone: 9/9 green.
